일시
장소
비고
1. 지원서 접수
(인터넷 접수만 시행)
2026. 9. 28.(월) 10:00 ~ 10. 2.(금) 17:00
입학홈페이지 - [일반대학원 공지사항 ]
사진파일(3×4cm) 첨부
2. 서류 제출
(지원서, 자기소개서 및 수학계획서 등 제외)
2026. 10. 6.(화) 17:00까지
지원 모집단위 소속 대학(원) 교무행정실
방문 또는 우편 제출
3. 수험표 출력
2026. 10. 6.(화) ~
입학홈페이지 - [일반대학원 공지사항 ]
면접 및 구술고사 시 지참

2026. 10. 12.(월) 13:00 ~
(※ 시작시간 변동 가능성 있음)
203동 208호
5. 합격자 발표
2026. 11. 19.(목) 18:00 이후
입학 홈페이지-일반대학원 공지사항 (admission.snu.ac.kr)
6. 합격자 등록
2027. 2. 1.(월) 10:00 ~ 2. 5.(금) 16:00
전국 농협, 신한은행, 우리은행 지점 (인터넷·스마트폰 뱅킹 가능)
입학홈페이지 - [일반대학원 공지사항 ] 에서 등록금고지서를 출력하여 납부
상기 일정은 학사일정 등에 따라 변경될 수 있음
지원자격
석사과정·석사박사통합과정
국내·외에서 학사학위를 취득한 자[2027년 2월(국외대학 졸업자는 2027년 3월)까지
취득 예정자 포함] 또는 법령에 따라 학사학위 이상의 학력이 있다고 인정된 자
TEPS 또는 TOEFL 정기시험에서 모집 단위별로 정한 일정 점수 이상의 성적을 취득한 자
지원가능 영어점수 (2027학년도 기준)
Drag from side to side.
TEPS 점수
TOEFL/IBT
학과(부)
개정 TEPS : 327점 이상 (기존 TEPS: 601점 이상)
96점(5.0) 이상
뇌인지과학과
TOEFL iBT 시험 응시일이 2026. 1. 21. 이후인 경우에는 개정된 TOEFL iBT 점수 체계 기준을 적용함
정기시험 성적만 인정함
인정되는 영어성적
지원서 접수 마감일(2026. 10. 2.) 기준 2년 이내에 응시하고 발표가 완료되어 입학지원서에 기재된 정기시험 성적(TOEFL My Best Scores* 및 Home Edition 성적 포함)
본교 석사학위자가 본교 대학원 입학 시 또는 논문 제출 자격시험에서 인정받은 성적
지원서 접수 마감일(2026. 10. 2.) 기준 10년 이내 응시 성적까지 인정하며, 해당 성적(응시 회차 및 점수) 을 입학지원서에 기재해야 함(소속 단과대학 교무행정실에 문의)
해당 점수는 현행 지원 가능 공인 영어시험성적 기준을 충족해야 함
영어권 국가 소재 대학(원) 졸업(예정)자
각 대학(원) 전형 위원회 심사 결과에 따라 영어성적 제출을 면제할 수 있음
각 대학(원)마다 영어성적 제출이 면제되는 영어권 국가가 다를 수 있으니, 지원 예정 대학(원)에 연락하여 확인해야 하며, 영어성적 제출이 면제된 최종 합격자는 해당 영어권 국가 소재 대학(원)의 졸업(학위)증명서 및 해당 증명서에 대한 아포스티유 또는 영사 확인을 제출해야 함
"영어권 국가"는 영어가 모국어이거나 법정 공용어인 국가를 뜻하며, 한국 소재 대학(원)은 포함되지 않음
박사과정
국내·외에서 석사학위를 취득한 자[2027년 2월(국외대학 졸업자는 2027년 3월)까지
취득 예정자 포함] 또는 법령에 따라 석사학위 이상의 학력이 있다고 인정된 자
모집 단위별 동종계열 지정 기준을 충족한 자
TEPS 또는 TOEFL 정기시험에서 모집 단위별로 정한 일정 점수 이상의 성적을 취득한 자
지원가능 영어점수
Drag from side to side.
TEPS 점수
TOEFL/IBT
학과(부)
개정 TEPS : 327점 이상 (기존 TEPS: 601점 이상)
96점(5.0) 이상
뇌인지과학과
TOEFL iBT 시험 응시일이 2026. 1. 21. 이후인 경우에는 개정된 TOEFL iBT 점수 체계 기준을 적용함
정기시험 성적만 인정함
인정되는 영어성적
지원서 접수 마감일(2026. 10. 2.) 기준 2년 이내에 응시하고 발표가 완료되어 입학지원서에 기재된 정기시험 성적(TOEFL My Best Scores* 및 Home Edition 성적 포함)
본교 석사학위자가 본교 대학원 입학 시 또는 논문 제출 자격시험에서 인정받은 성적 지원서 접수 마감일(2026. 10. 2.) 기준 10년 이내 응시 성적까지 인정하며, 해당 성적(응시 회차 및 점수) 을 입학지원서에 기재해야 함(소속 단과대학 교무행정실에 문의) 해당 점수는 현행 지원 가능 공인 영어시험성적 기준을 충족해야 함
영어권 국가 소재 대학(원) 졸업(예정)자
각 대학(원) 전형 위원회 심사 결과에 따라 영어성적 제출을 면제할 수 있음
각 대학(원)마다 영어성적 제출이 면제되는 영어권 국가가 다를 수 있으니, 지원 예정 대학(원)에 연락하여 확인해야 하며, 영어성적 제출이 면제된 최종 합격자는 해당 영어권 국가 소재 대학(원)의 졸업(학위)증명서 및 해당 증명서에 대한 아포스티유 또는 영사 확인을 제출해야 함
"영어권 국가"는 영어가 모국어이거나 법정 공용어인 국가를 뜻하며, 한국 소재 대학(원)은 포함되지 않음
면접 및 구술고사
일시 및 장소 : 2026. 10. 12.(월) 13:00 ~ (※ 시작시간 변동 가능성 있음) (장소: 203동 208호)
화상면접 관련 안내 :
외국 거주자 또는 특수상황에 있는 자에 한하여 지원서 접수 시 화상 면접을 신청할 수 있음. 단, 화상 면접진행 여부는 지원 모집 단위가 속한 대학(원)에서 결정하므로 반드시 사전에 해당 대학(원)과 협의하여야 함
화상 면접에 응시하여 합격한 자는 추후 증빙자료(출입국사실증명서, 진단서 등)를 제출하여야 하며, 허위 사유나 타인에 의한 면접 등 부정한 방법으로 화상 면접에 응시한 경우 합격 또는 입학을 취소함
지원서 접수(인터넷)
일시
2026. 9. 28.(월) 10:00 ~ 10. 2.(금) 17:00
접수방법
서울대학교 입학홈페이지 - [일반대학원 공지사항에서 확인 ]
유의사항
지원서 접수마감시간[2026. 10. 2.(금)] 17:00까지 전형료 결제를 완료하여야 접수가 인정되며, 추가 접수는 진행하지 않습니다. 접수기간 중 지원자의 접속환경 등을 미리 확인하시기 바랍니다.
지원서 접수(전형료 결제) 완료 후에는 과정 및 모집단위를 변경할 수 없으며, 접수취소 및 전형료 환불이 불가하므로, 접수 시 모집안내 등 지원에 필요한 사항을 반드시 확인한 후 지원서를 작성하시기바랍니다.
개인인적사항, 자기소개서는 접수마감 시까지 수정 가능

합격자 발표
일시 : 2026. 11. 19.(목) 18:00 이후
장소 : 서울대학교 입학 홈페이지(http://admission.snu.ac.kr ) (서울대학교 ⇒ 입학 ⇒ 일반대학원 공지사항 ⇒ 합격자발표 ⇒ 성명, 생년월일과 수험번호로 검색)
지원자 유의사항
2개 이상의 모집단위에 중복 지원할 수 없으며, 중복 지원 시 전체 모집단위에 대하여 불합격 처리됩니다.
Applicants are not permitted to apply to more than one department during current admission (paying application fee and receiving their application number more than once) and will be disqualified for all applications if found doing so.
지원인원이 모집인원에 미달하더라도 수학에 지장이 있다고 판단되는 자에 대하여는 선발하지 않을 수 있습니다.
Even if the number of applicants falls short of the admissions quota, those who are judged to have difficulties in studying may not be selected.
입학고사 성적은 공개하지 않습니다.
The Admissions Test scores will not be disclosed.
접수된 입학지원서 및 서류(인터넷 제출 포함)는 취소, 정정 또는 반환(환불)할 수 없으며, 접수기간 마감 전·후에 동일하게 적용합니다.
Once submitted, the application form and all supporting documents (including those submitted online) cannot be canceled, modified, or returned (refunded), and this policy applies equally both before and after the application deadline.
입학지원서 기재사항 허위기재, 서류의 위·변조 및 기타 부정한 방법으로 지원하거나 합격한 사실이 발견될 시 합격 및 입학을 취소하며, 향후 본교 입학에 제한을 받을 수 있습니다.
If it is found that the applicant applied or has been accepted to the University using false information on the application form, forgery of documents, or other fraudulent methods, admission decision will be cancelled and future admission to the University may be restricted.
수험생 유의사항을 준수하여야 하고, 특히 답안지의 특정 부분에 인적사항 등 신분을 노출할 수 있는 사항을 표기할 수 없습니다.
Candidates must follow the instructions carefully and should NOT include detailed personal information (names, occupations, etc.) in certain sections of the answer sheet.
선택과목(제2외국어 포함)은 입학지원서에 기재한 과목을 응시하여야 하며, 응시과목과 입학지원서의 선택과목이 다를 경우 이를 무효로 처리합니다.
For elective courses (including secondary foreign language), students must take the courses listed on the application form. If students take elective courses other than the ones they listed in the application form, they will be invalidated.
TEPS, TOEFL 및 제2외국어시험 성적 미달, 기타 결격사유에 해당할 경우에도 전형료 및 제출서류는 반환하지 않습니다.(단, 전형료 반환에 관한 사항은 “입학전형료 반환” 참조)
The application fee and submitted documents will not be returned even if the applicants were rejected due to failure to meet TEPS, TOEFL, or secondary foreign language test scores, or other reasons (Please refer to the section regarding waivers or refunds of application fee)
제2외국어시험 응시자는 사전을 지참하여 활용할 수 있습니다.(단, 전자사전은 불가)
Applicants who take secondary foreign language test can bring their own dictionary (However, electronic dictionary is not allowed).
석사과정 사범계열에 입학하여 교원자격증 취득을 희망하는 경우 학사과정에서 표시과목 관련 학과를 졸업하고 소정의 전공과목과 교직과목 및 4주간의 현장실습을 이수하여야 합니다.(교원양성혁신센터 홈페이지 참조 및 자세한 사항은 지원 모집단위(학과)로 문의)
Those who are admitted to Master’s course in College of Education and want to obtain a Teaching Certificate have to obtain a Bachelor’s degree from the related departments, complete relevant major courses and teaching courses, and complete 4 weeks of field experience. (Refer to Link for detailed information and please directly contact corresponding department for any inquiries)
합격자로서 2027년 2월(국외대학 졸업자는 2027년 3월)까지 신입생 입학자격을 갖추지 못한 경우 또는 합격자 등록기간에 등록을 하지 않거나 소정서류를 제출하지 않은 경우 합격(입학)을 취소합니다.
Admitted applicants who fail to either meet the entrance qualifications, register during the registration period, or submit designated documents by February 2027(March 2027 in case of graduates of international university) will be disqualified
서울대학교 학칙 제70조에 의거 이중학적은 금지되므로 본교 입학 후 둘 이상의 본교 대학(원) 또는 타 대학교에 동시에 재학 또는 휴학할 수 없습니다. 이를 위반하는 경우 합격 및 입학을 취소할 수 있습니다.
In accordance with Article 70 of the SNU School Rules, dual registration is strictly prohibited. Applicants risk disqualification if they attend or take leave of absence from more than one SNU graduate program or any other universities while being registered at SNU. It is imperative that applicants resolve any existing registration with other university programs before enrolling at SNU.
합격자등록 및 제출서류
합격자 등록
기 간 : 2027. 2. 1.(월) 10:00 ~ 2. 5.(금) 16:00 예정
장 소 : 전국 소재 농협, 신한은행 및 우리은행 지점(인터넷.스마트폰뱅킹 가능)
등록금액 : 납입고지서로 고지함
등록금고지서 교부 : 서울대학교 입학홈페이지 일반대학원 공지사항에서 직접 출력
지정된 기간 중 등록하지 않는 경우 합격이 취소됩니다.
제출서류
2027년 2월 말까지(세부일정 추후 안내) ☞ 합격자 소속 대학(원) 교무행정실(자연과학대학은 소속 학과 또는 학부)로 제출
Drag from side to side.
제출서류
비고
학사학위 졸업증명서 1부 (석사·석박통합과정 합격자),
석사학위 졸업증명서 1부 (박사과정 합격자)
원본서류 제출 필수,
아포스티유 제출 대상(외국대학(원) 졸업)은 표 하단 ※ 참조
외국대학 졸업자 중 학사학위가 외국대학으로 박사학위의 영어성적을 면제 받을 경우 학사학위 아포스티유를 제출해야함
출입국사실증명서 또는 진단서 등 1부
화상면접에 응시하여 합격한 자에 한함
합격자 중 외국대학(원) 졸업자(영어성적 제출을 면제받은 경우 포함)는 아포스티유 가입국가인 경우 아포스티유 확인서를 함께 제출하여야 하며 아포스티유 미가입국은 국외교육기관확인서 또는 영사확인을 함께 제출
국가별 아포스티유 관련기관 정보 : [www.hcch.net](https://www.hcch.net) - Members & Parties - HCCH Members 참고
한국어나 영어로 작성되지 않은 서류는 공증받은 한글 또는 영문 번역본을 첨부해야 함
중국 대학(원) 졸업자는 졸업증명서 및 학위증명서의 영사확인 또는 아포스티유를 중국 교육부 학위인증센터(CHSI)의 Online Verification Report of Student Record와 함께 제출([www.chsi.com.cn](https://www.chsi.com.cn) 참조)
특별한 사유 없이 지정된 기일 내에 제출하지 않을 경우 합격이 취소될 수 있습니다.
기타 안내사항
외국어(영어, 제2외국어) 입학(고사) 성적의 논문제출자격시험 인정
모집단위별 소정 점수 이상을 제출(취득)한 자는 학위수여규정에 따라 논문제출자격시험에서 해당 과목을 합격으로 인정받을 수 있습니다.
석사·박사통합과정 안내
석사․박사통합과정은 석사학위 및 박사학위의 과정이 통합된 것으로 중도 포기시 학칙 및 규정이 정하는 기준에 따라 석사학위 수여가 가능합니다.
전형 세부사항(절차, 일정) 안내

지도교수선정
학사/교육
학과소개
교수/연구
학사/교육
입학/교육생
커뮤니티
학사안내
학사안내
개설교과목
대학원생활
장학제도
지도교수선정
학사일정
등록/학적
수강신청
지도교수선정
학위이수요건
학위논문제출
지도교수 선정 시기 :
입학 후 2개월 내에 결정한다.
희망지도교수신청 :
의과학과 소속 교수 중에서 희망 지도교수 3인을 선택하고 희망 순위(1, 2, 3)와 함께 지도 교수 선정 신청서에 기입하여 의과학과에 제출한다.
지도교수선정 신청서 제출 :
신청서 제출은 매 학년도 3월초, 9월 초에 제출하며 제출 자료를 가지고 대학원 학사위원회를 개최하여 지도교수를 선정하여 통보한다.
지도교수 자격 :
지도교수는 의과학과 소속 교수이어야 하며, 박사학위를 소지한 조교수(기금조교수 포함) 이상으로 한다.
지도교수 변경 :
지도교수 변경은 입학 후 1년 이내에 변경 전 교수와 변경 후 교수 양쪽의 승인에 의해 가능하다.
(학칙 점검). 천재지변 혹은 지도교수의 이직에 의한 변경은 1년 이후에도 가능하다.
임시지도교수 :
지도교수가 결정되기 전까지 신입생의 학사지도는 임시 지도교수의 자격으로 의과학과 학과장이 담당한다.
연구실 순환실습 (Lab rotation) :
석박통합 신입생의 경우 의과학과 내 2~3개 연구실을 순환하는 연구실 순환실습에 참여한다. 이를 통해 의과학과 내 다양한 연구실을 경험하고, 지도교수를 결정하지 못한 학생들은 지도교수 탐색의 기회로 활용할 수 있다.
연구실 순환실습 연구실 선정 :
합격자 발표 직후 (2월, 8월), 안내에 따라 순환실습 신청서를 의과학과에 제출하면 개강 전 연구실을 배정받는다.
연구실 순환실습 시기 :
입학 후 2개월간 (3-4월, 9-10월) 시행하고, 신입생 필수교과목인 '의과학강독'과 병행하여 실시한다.
ENGLISH

입학/교육생
입학 FAQ
입학 FAQ
입학/교육생
학과소개
교수/연구
학사/교육
입학/교육생
커뮤니티
입학 FAQ
모집공고
입학 FAQ
학부 연구 교육생
Q 추천서도 4부를 내야 하나요?
추천서의 경우, 추천인이 밀봉하여 보낸 상태라면 1부를 보내시면 됩니다.
Q 본교 대학원 석사를 졸업하였는데, 박사과정 지원시 TEPS 점수를 제출해야 하나요?
추가로 영어 성적을 제출하실 필요 없습니다.
서울대학교 대학원에 공식적으로 제출한 영어성적의 경우 2년의 유효기간을 적용하지 않습니다. 즉, 석사과정 입학시 551점 이상의 텝스점수(개정 점수는 298점 이상)를 제출한 경우 본교 박사과정 지원시 ‘석사과정 입학시에 인정받았던 텝스 점수’로 지원할 수 있습니다.
Q 면접 및 구술고사는 어떻게 진행되나요?
구술고사는 의과학과의 주요 연구분야에 대한 질문 15개가 면접 직전에 제시되며, 이중 지원자가 가장 자신 있는 3개 문제를 선택하여 답변하는 방식입니다. 구술고사와 면접이 함께 진행되며 지원자 1인당 소요시간은 약 10~15분입니다.
Q 기출문제나 전공범위를 알 수 있나요?
기출 문제 및 전공범위를 공개하지 않습니다. 의과학과 주요 연구 분야와 관련된 전공 지식 및 지원 희망하는 연구 분야에 대하여 준비하시면 될 것입니다.
Q 입학 시험전에 희망 지도교수를 미리 면담하거나 결정해야 하나요?
입학시험 전에 관심 있는 분야의 교수님을 방문하여 희망 연구분야에 대한 상담을 받으시면 세부 전공 분야를 선택하시는데 도움을 받을 수 있습니다. 물론 사전면접 여부는 입학시험의 합격 여부에는 영향을 주지 않습니다.
Q 그렇다면 입학후 지도교수 선정은 어떻게 진행되나요?
입학시험 전에 관심 분야의 교수님을 방문하여 희망 연구분야에 대한 상담을 받으시면 세부 전공 분야를 선택하시는데 도움을 받을 수 있습니다. 물론 사전면접 여부는 입학시험의 합격 여부에는 영향을 주지 않습니다.
Q 대학원 의과학과에 입학하면 병역특례를 받을 수 있나요?
네, 있습니다. 의과학과는 ‘자연계대학원’에 포함되므로, ‘대학연구기관 전문연구요원 편입대상자 선발시험’에 합격하면 전문연구요원으로 편입, 병역특례를 받을 수 있습니다. 의무종사기간을 포함 박사학위 취득까지 최소 5년이 소요됩니다(의무종사기간 3년)
Q 석사과정 후에 박사과정에 지원하고, 전문연구요원으로 병역특례를 받기까지 몇 년이 소요되나요?
학부졸업자의 경우는 석박통합과정 및 전문연구요원으로의 병역특례를 모두 마치기까지 최소 6년이 소요됩니다. 석사졸업자는 최소 5년입니다.
1) 석박통합(3년) + 전문연구요원(3년) = 6년
2) 박사(2년) + 전문연구요원(3년) = 5년
‘ 석박통합과정(석사학위를 거치지 않고 석,박사 학위과정을 통합하여 박사학위를 수여하는 제도)’를 이용하면 수료연한을 최대1년 단축할 수 있으므로 최소 3년 내에 박사학위를 취득하실 수 있습니다. 전문연구요원은 박사학위과정(수료)를 제외한 의무종사기간이 3년입니다.
Q 장학금혜택은 어떻게 받을 수 있나요?
교내 장학금 신청시 수업료 10%에 해당하는 금액(약 500,000원)을 지원받을 수 있으며, 지도교수 선정 후 연구실의 연구비 지원을 받으실 수 있습니다. 자세한 장학금 종류는 ‘교육-장학제도’를 확인하시기 바라며, 장학금 지원규모 및 지원여부는 매년 변동될 수 있습니다.
Q 직업을 가지고 있는데 입학할 수 있나요?
대학원 의과학과는 전일제로 운영되고 있으므로 직업(인턴,레지던트,유(무)급펠로우 등 임상활동 포함)과 학업을 병행할 수 없습니다. 석박통합과정의 경우 3년, 박사과정의 경우 2년으로 의무전일제기간을 두고 있으며, 이 기간 동안은 직업을 가지실 수 없습니다. 자세한 사항은 의과학과 사무실에 문의하십시오.
ENGLISH

Doctoral dissertation: within 6 years after completion of Doctoral course.

Student who plans to present a doctoral dissertation must submit the form (refer to announcement of the department) with 1 copy of summary and a confirmation letter from supervising professor, in accordance with the ‘Rule on Doctoral Degree Conferment (publishing in international journals), Department of Biological Sciences’.

Alternating Current (AC) Electrolysis toward Organic Syntheses
RECENT PUBLICATIONS
PHANTOM platform integrating photothermal PCR and swCNT-FET for rapid molecular diagnostics
Plasmonic photothermal polymerase chain reaction (PPT-PCR) is a nucleic acid amplification technique that utilizes the localized surface plasmon resonance effect of plasmonic nanomaterials under the irradiation of light with specific wavelengths to achieve rapid thermal cycling. PPT-PCR is considered as a next-generation PCR technique due to the potential to be applied to the development of point-of-care diagnostics and the fast, sensitive and accurate detection performance. In this study, we present PHANTOM, a proof-of-concept system that functionally integrates plasmonic photothermal PCR, magnetic nanoparticle removal, and swCNT-FET-based label-free electrical readout for rapid molecular diagnostics. The swCNT-FET sensor exhibits high sensitivity, capable of detecting low concentrations of target nucleic acids within minutes after PPT-PCR amplification. Besides, to improve the specificity of the assay, we introduce a hairpin structured primer to generate amplicons with an external single-strand tail that can hybridize with probes modified on the swCNT-FET sensor. With this design, an estimated limit of detection of 1.5 aM (experimentally validated down to 10 aM) was achieved within 20 min.
2026-11-15
Polyacrylic acid-functionalized MXene organohydrogels for high-performance and durable wearable sensors
Practical applications of MXene-based hydrogels have been limited owing to their poor oxidation stability and mechanical performance. To overcome these issues, numerous efforts have been devoted to the surface modification of MXenes, but the optimal type of surface modifier has not been systematically explored. Herein, MXene is modified with diverse polymeric modifiers, including polyphenols (PPs) and linear synthetic polymers (LSPs), and the resulting properties are investigated in relation to their molecular structures. LSPs outperform PPs in improving oxidation resistance and mechanical performance of hydrogels, among which poly(acrylic acid) (PAA) provides the highest overall performance. Subsequently, the PAA-modified MXene hydrogel (PMH) is converted into organohydrogels (PMOH) to enhance mechanical performance and environmental durability. The PMOH exhibits substantially improved mechanical performance, including a tensile strain of 1487.5%, a tensile strength of 750 kPa, and a toughness of 5.27 MJ m(-3) with a tissue-like modulus of 71.9 kPa. The PMOH also shows enhanced conductivity compared with the bare organohydrogel (0.105 to 0.133 S m(-1)), robust adhesion to the skin (55.3 kPa), and outstanding resistance to freezing, dehydration, and oxidation. Owing to these properties, the PMOH is employed as a wearable strain and electrophysiological sensor. This study provides rational insights into the development of durable and high-performance MXene hydrogels for biomedical applications.
2026-11-01
Rational Fe-4d dual-atom pairing for stabilizing redox mediators in lithium-oxygen batteries
Lithium-oxygen batteries (LOBs) offer exceptionally high energy density but are limited by poor reversibility and large charging overpotentials owing to sluggish lithium oxide decomposition. We propose a rational Fe-4d dualatom catalyst (DAC) strategy to regulate oxygen evolution and stabilize NO2- redox mediation. Fe-Zr/Nb/Mo pairs are atomically dispersed onto defect-rich nitrogen-doped carbon nanotubes (NCNTs) and evaluated as oxygen-electrode catalysts. Among these, Nb-Fe-NCNT exhibits a superior performance, enabling excellent cycling for over 290 cycles with significantly reduced polarization. Electrochemical analysis reveals that Nb-Fe enhances charge-transfer kinetics and sustains low-voltage redox-mediated oxygen evolution while suppressing high-voltage direct Li2O2 oxidation. Ex situ characterization confirms highly reversible Li2O2 formation and decomposition. Density functional theory shows favorable reaction energetics and enhanced electron redistribution, which strengthens mediator interaction. These results demonstrate that metal-dependent electronic synergy in Fe-4d DACs governs oxygen-evolution pathways and provide practical design guidelines for durable, nonprecious catalysts for high-performance LOBs.
2026-10-15
A positive feedback loop between NPR1 and SOG1 reinforces genotoxic tolerance in Arabidopsis
Cellular functions and viability of living organisms are constantly challenged by endogenous and environmental agents that induce DNA damage. In plants, DNA-damaging agents, such as salicylic acid (SA), and the double-strand-break-inducing antibiotic Zeocin, trigger the DNA damage response, thereby promoting genotoxic tolerance. We found that the SA-responsive gene NONEXPRESSOR OF PATHOGENESIS-RELATED GENES 1 (NPR1) and the DNA-damage-responsive gene SUPPRESSOR OF GAMMA RESPONSE 1 (SOG1) are both essential for genotoxic tolerance. Mutants lacking either NPR1 or SOG1 exhibit reduced tolerance to genotoxic stress. Furthermore, NPR1 and SOG1 form a positive feedback loop at the transcriptional level, thereby amplifying genotoxic stress responses. Additionally, NPR1 and SOG1 physically interact and co-occupy the NAC085 promoter, synergistically activating its expression and consequently enhancing genotoxic tolerance. Genetic analysis revealed that NAC085 acts downstream of NPR1 and SOG1 to control genotoxic tolerance. Taken together, our results demonstrate that a positive feedback loop between NPR1 and SOG1 promotes NAC085 expression, thereby enhancing genotoxic tolerance and safeguarding genome integrity in plants.
2026-10-12
Calcium-Specific-Binding PLGA Microspheres with Embedded BMP-2 for Bone Regeneration
Bone morphogenetic protein-2 (BMP-2) is a potent osteoinductive factor for bone regeneration, but its rapid clearance and dose-dependent adverse effects limit its clinical use. This study developed calcium-binding poly(butyl methacrylate-co-methacryloyloxyethyl phosphate) (PBMP)-coated poly(lactide-co-glycolide) (PLGA) microparticles to enhance calcium-rich surface anchoring and enable sustained BMP-2 release. Microparticles were characterized for morphology, zeta potential, encapsulation efficiency, release kinetics, and hydroxyapatite (HA)-binding affinity, and were further evaluated for in vivo efficacy. PBMP coating markedly increased the HA-binding affinity of PLGA microparticles. Encapsulation efficiency was over 55%, and BMP-2 was released in a sustained manner over 30 days, with the cumulative release gradually approaching a plateau during the later incubation period. When applied to a rat calvarial defect model, the PBMP-coated microparticle (BMP-2-PLGA/PBMP) group showed the greatest bone volume fraction, bone surface area, and trabecular number, along with the lowest trabecular separation compared to the free BMP-2 and control groups. Histological analysis confirmed denser and more mature bone matrix formation in the BMP-2-PLGA/PBMP group. These findings demonstrate that PBMP-coated PLGA microparticles provide HA-targeted, sustained BMP-2 delivery, resulting in improved bone regeneration and microarchitecture. This delivery platform represents a promising strategy for more effective bone defect repair.
2026-10-12
Dopant Engineering of Isostructural Metal Nanoclusters for Modulating CO2 Electroreduction
Achieving atomic-level insight into how dopants regulate the electrocatalytic CO2 reduction reaction (eCO2RR) of metal nanoparticles is challenging due to the lack of well-defined, compositionally tunable, and structurally identical model systems. Herein, we report two isostructural nanoclusters: [Au10Ag4(DPPP)5Cl4]2+ (Au10Ag4) and [Au9Ag2Cu3(DPPP)5Cl4]2+ (Au9Ag2Cu3), which differ only in metal composition (Au & horbar;Ag vs. Au & horbar;Ag & horbar;Cu). Together with the isostructural monometallic [Au14(DPPP)5Br4]2+ (Au14), they serve as ideal platforms to elucidate dopant effects on eCO2RR. Electrochemical studies reveal that Ag doping (Au10Ag4) suppresses both CO selectivity and activity of Au14, whereas further Cu incorporation (Au9Ag2Cu3) not only enhances these metrics relative to Au10Ag4 but also surpasses Au14. Specifically, Au9Ag2Cu3 exhibits an enhanced CO Faradaic efficiency (FECO, 73.7%-95.5%), exceeding that of Au14 (63.6%-95.0%) and Au10Ag4 (66.2%-79.5%), and its CO partial current densities were improved by 1.5- and 2.6-fold compared to those of Au14 and Au10Ag4, respectively. Theoretical calculations and in situ experiments reveal that Ag & horbar;Cu co-doping lowers the energy barrier for *COOH formation and weakens CO binding, promoting CO2-to-CO conversion. In contrast, Ag doping alone raises the energy barrier for *COOH formation and impedes CO desorption, diminishing catalytic activity. These findings provide atomistic insights into dopant-regulated eCO2RR and inspire the rational design of advanced alloy electrocatalysts.
2026-10-12
The RLK7-MPK3/6-LEAFY PETIOLE signaling axis mediates growth inhibition during PIP peptide-induced immunity in Arabidopsis
Pathogen-associated molecular patterns (PAMPs) activate plant immunity through plasma membrane-localized receptor-like kinases (RLKs) and receptor-like proteins. The PAMPflg22 binds FLAGELLIN SENSING 2 (FLS2), inducing the expression of defense-related genes, including those encoding PAMP-INDUCED PEPTIDEs (PIPs), which enhance pathogen resistance via RLK7. However, the mechanisms underlying PAMP-and PIP-mediated growth-defense trade-off remain unclear. Here, we show that PIP1 and PIP2 suppress lateral root (LR) development and seedling growth through the RLK7-MPK3/6-LEAFY PETIOLE (LEP) signaling cascade, with LEP acting as a key node that inhibits LR formation and seedling growth. Exogenous application of PIP1/2 peptides and overexpression of prePIP1 and prePIP2 inhibit LR development, whereas mutations in prePIP1 or prePIP2 enhance primary root growth and accelerate LR develomental kinetics. Genetic analyses, coimmunoprecipitation, peptide-binding and kinase assays, rapamycin-induced dimerization, and a BIR3 chimera system collectively revealed that PIP1/2 recognition promotes the interaction of RLK7 with SOMATIC EMBRYOGENESIS RECEPTOR KINASE coreceptors, triggering MPK3/6-dependent phosphorylation events and inhibiting LR development. The transcription factor LEP acts downstream of RLK7-MPK6 signaling to inhibit LR development. Loss of LEP function alleviates PIP-induced inhibition of seedling growth and LR formation without compromising immune responses. These findings suggest that PIP1/2 peptides mediate the trade-off between growth and defense via the RLK7-MPK6-LEP signaling axis. The flg22 peptide suppresses LR formation and seedling growth in part through the PIP1/2-RLK7-MPK6-LEP signaling pathway, which bifurcates from immune responses downstream of MPK3/6. This study establishes a genetic framework for optimizing crop resilience by decoupling immune activation from growth suppression.
2026-10-12
Nonmonotonic Li-Content Evolution and Size-Dependent Lithium Accessibility in Degraded High-Nickel Cathodes
Understanding how degradation reshapes lithium (de)insertion pathways in high-nickel cathodes is crucial for extending battery lifetime. Here, we integrate operando synchrotron X-ray diffraction (sXRD) with electrode-level transmission X-ray microscopy (TXM) to quantitatively resolve how degradation redistributes lithium accessibility in Ni-rich NCA cathodes. Using a 26-state deconvolution framework, we show that degraded electrodes exhibit nonmonotonic Li-content population evolution: delithiation kinetics are initially suppressed, transiently synchronized, and ultimately diverge, leaving similar to 7% discharge-like states and similar to 13% intermediate Li-content states at the end of charge. Spatially resolved TXM reveals that this divergence originates from size-dependent accessibility constraints-large particles develop persistent intra-particle state-of-charge gradients, while small particles exhibit pronounced inter-particle disparity. This work establishes a quantitative framework linking bulk Li-content population dynamics to particle-level degradation mechanisms in high-nickel cathodes.
2026-10-09
Bidirectional Alkene-Nucleophile Coupling at a Single Carbon Center Enabled by a Methylene-Bridged Thianthrenium Dication
The chemical space explored through synthesis is largely shaped by the availability of building blocks supplied by nature. Because biosynthesis follows defined metabolic logic, naturally occurring compounds are unevenly distributed across carbon count, creating intrinsic biases in synthetic accessibility. Consequently, molecules differing by only a single carbon atom can require markedly different synthetic effort. To overcome this longstanding challenge, we report a bidirectional coupling strategy that assembles alkenes and nucleophiles across an intervening methylene unit. In contrast to conventional alkene hydrofunctionalization, which joins these partners directly, this approach retains the modularity of the parent reaction while increasing product carbon count. We show that a designer methylene-bridged thianthrenium dication reagent exploits distinct redox properties and strain-release reactivity to differentiate two C-S bonds at a single carbon center. This design enables sequential alkene hydroalkylation and downstream nucleophile incorporation via substitution, cross-coupling or radical manifolds. This divergent reactivity accommodates diverse nucleophile classes (N, O, B, S, Se, C and halides), facilitates the hybridization of complex biorelevant fragments and expands retrosynthetic flexibility in the synthesis of marketed drugs. By rerouting two abundant substrate classes through a single-carbon linchpin, this platform opens one-carbon-shifted chemical space beyond the reach of conventional alkene hydrofunctionalization.
2026-10-07
Disentangling Catalytic Activity and Film Conductivity Reveals Temporally Decoupled Catalyst Evolution and Conductivity Artifacts
Catalytic activity and electrical conductivity are critical to the performance of electrocatalysts and electrochemical devices, yet their combined assessment under operating conditions remains challenging. Here, we introduce operando Fourier-transform electrochemical source-drain impedance spectroscopy (FT-ESDIS), which simultaneously measures catalyst-film conductivity and charge-transfer resistance (R ct) with high temporal resolution at high current densities. During Fe incorporation into nickel (oxy)hydroxide (NiO x H y ), FT-ESDIS revealed that catalytic activation and charge transport properties evolve on distinct time scales, demonstrating that activity alone cannot capture catalyst-layer evolution during operation. At higher overpotentials, nickel-containing (oxy)hydroxides (Ni(Fe)O x H y ) showed an abrupt apparent conductivity increase upon oxidation, with conductivity remaining nearly unchanged thereafter, whereas iron (oxy)hydroxide (FeO x H y ) exhibited a gradual increase in apparent conductivity. Previous reports attributed this overpotential-dependent increase to the formation of high-valent Fe species. However, we show that the increase arises predominantly from an electrolyte-mediated current artifact, in which a potential perturbation at one electrode changes the local solution potential at the neighboring electrode surface and modulates its Faradaic current, producing an apparent conductivity response without direct through-film transport. We further establish a quantitative relationship between this artifact and R ct, enabling its estimation and subtraction from the apparent conductivity. The corrected FeO x H y conductivity exhibits substantially weaker potential dependence, suggesting that charge transport involving high-valent Fe species, if present, contributes much less than inferred from the uncorrected response. By resolving kinetic and transport properties under operating conditions, FT-ESDIS provides a more comprehensive basis for interpreting electrocatalyst behavior and establishes transport and reactivity as complementary descriptors for electrochemical energy-conversion systems.

DEPARTMENT OF CHEMISTRY, SEOUL NATIONAL UNIVERSITY.
Home
Admissions
FAQ
Home
Admissions
FAQ
FAQ
1. Why should I consider SNU chemistry?
The graduate school of chemistry at SNU has a long tradition of the contribution to science and societies by educating the brightest minds in chemistry. The alumni of the graduate school of chemistry have grown to 2000 M.S. and 780 Ph.Ds who have taken the leading roles in educational and research institutions, public sectors, and commercial institutions. When you join SNU chemistry, you will be a part of this tradition of excellence by preparing to be a world leader in the progress of chemical sciences.
The school of chemistry is recognized as a nation’s top research institution in chemistry and chemical sciences, which has been evaluated as one of the world-leading chemistry department by the global ranking indices such as QS world university ranking and nature index. SNU chemistry has been selected as a recipient of the BK21 program, the award for the nation’s top departments of graduate education.
SNU provides a diverse and yet intimate academic environment in the downtown Seoul, one of the cultural centers in the globe. At SNU, students will enjoy cultural and academic diversity. This experience will make our students to be prepared to lead the world and serve the community.
2. How about the faculty and research areas?
The chemistry faculty consists of 36 world-leading chemists who study all areas of chemistry. SNU chemistry provide a world-class research experience to our students in chemistry and related fields. Chemistry department and SNU operates the state-of-the art instruments and research facilities. The faculty and graduate students are conducting researches in all areas of chemistry including the origin of life, the nature of chemical bonding, the understanding of molecular structures, the development of new chemical reactions, pharmaceutical chemistry, materials chemistry, and energy and environmental chemistry.
3. How to prepare for the application?
the applicant must submit the complete package including the statement of purpose, essay and research statement. The admission committee evaluate applicants based on their academic performance (GPA), the grades of the chemistry-related courses, and the application package. The academic performance, research experience, research statement, and the statement of purpose are important factors for admission. However, the evaluation will not rely on a single factor. Therefore, applicants are encouraged to make detailed but concise statements delivering the purpose and plan for the graduate study.
4. How critical is the language proficiency test for admission?
All applicants for the graduate school must submit the English proficiency test score such as TOEFL, IELTS, and TEPS. For successful admission to Chemistry, the test score should meet the minimum requirements. Currently, the minimum requirements are 86 or higher for TOEFL (IBT) and 327 or higher for TEPS. English proficiency test scores will only be used as a requirement for admission. Higher scores will not be a deciding factor for admission.
5. On-site interview
On-site interview will be given to the applicant who passed the first round. Interviewers question applicants’ basic knowledge of college-level chemistry as well as their motivation and plan for graduate study.
6. How about the diversity of the student body
Expanding the diversity of students and faculty is the primary goal of the school of chemistry. As of 2020, 70 M.S students (including MS-PhD students who spent less than 18 months from their admission) and 247 pHD students are enrolled in the school. 69% of graduate students are male and 31% are female. 55% of our graduate students came from the universities and colleges throughout the country. 5% of students joined the school from abroad.
7. How important is the pre-admission contact to faculty members
The school encourage prospective student to freely contact faculty members for asking information about the graduate study and research at SNU. We are willing to provide information to students who want to be informed about the graduate school and admission, research opportunities offered by the school of chemistry. We would like to encourage all interested students to get familiar to the school and SNU. We remind any interested student that the pre-admission contact is only to provide information. The pre-admission contact will not be considered as a necessary step for admission.
Students may contact individual faculty members or administrative staff (ej0905@snu.ac.kr) for more information.
8. Is the previous research experience necessary for admission?
We provide a research internship programs to undergraduate students in June and December. To expand the internship opportunities for students in Korea, the school plans to establish new internship program for external students in vacation. Also, individual research labs may hire research interns. We encourage all interested students to apply for our internship programs. Please note that research experience alone will not be a deciding factor for admission.
9. How to decide supervisors
All applicants are required to address their research interests, interested research areas and research labs in their statement and research plan. You may choose multiple research areas and labs. After admission, students can choose their supervisors after interview with faculty members.
10. Financial support
The school of chemistry guarantees full financial support for graduate students. The support includes tuitions and fees, housing, and living expenses. Currently, the tuition is 38 M KRW (~ 3500 USD) per semester. Enrolled students are eligible for scholarships awarded by SNU and external foundations.
11. Housing
SNU provides student housing options for graduate students. The student apartment offers single-student units and shared units, which can host most of graduate students. The university also offers family housing. There could be a waiting list for single-student. For information, check the website of the student housing. Currently, the monthly rent for a shared unit is 214,200 ~ 247,000 KRW (200 ~ 230 USD). The community around SNU offers a plenty of rooms and houses.
12. Teaching assistantship
Teaching assistantship is a part of graduate education in the school of chemistry. From teaching a class, graduate students will have a valuable experience in education and communication. Therefore, all graduate students are required for teaching one lab class (chemistry lab) and serving a regular class as a teaching assistant.

서울대학교 컴퓨터공학부 AI 사용 지침
컴퓨터공학부는 교과목에서의 생성형 AI 활용 기준을 담은 「서울대학교 컴퓨터공학부 AI 사용 지침」을 제정하여 공개합니다.​AI는 이미 프로그래밍과 학습의 일상적인 도구가 되었습니다. 그러나 같은 학부 안에서도 과제마다 가르치고 평가하려는 역량이 다르기 때문에, 하나의 규칙으로 허용과 금지를 가르기는 어렵습니다. 이 지침은 AI 사용을 일률적으로 막는 대신, 과목과 과제가 무엇을 평가하는지에 따라 네 가지 사용 등급 중 하나를 정하도록 하여, 어디까지 AI를 활용해도 되는지를 학생과 교수가 미리 분명히 알 수 있게 하는 데 목적이 있습니다. 전문은 첨부한 PDF를 참고해 주십시오.​
2026년 인권/성평등 교육(법정의무교육) 이수 안내
4대 폭력예방교육(성희롱·성폭력·가정폭력·성매매)은 관련 법령에 따라 실시되는 법정의무교육으로 대학 전 구성원은 매년 1회 이상 교육을 이수하여야 합니다. ※ 본교에서는 "인권/성평등 교육"으로 통합·운영하고 있음.2026년 성평등가족부 "폭력예방교육 운영안내 지침" 강화에 따라 교내 구성원의 교육 이수율 달성 기준 및 교육 운영 요건은 다음과 같습니다.대상기준학생학부생, 대학원생(휴학생 제외)50% 이상 이수다음과 같이 온·오프라인 인권/성평등 교육을 지원하고 있으니 이수하여 주시기 바랍니다.○ 이수방법 - 온라인 교육 : 온라인 인권/성평등 교육 시스템(https://helplms.snu.ac.kr)에서 이수 - 맞춤형 교육 : 5인 이상 상시 신청 가능(대면) ※ 오프라인 인권/성평등 교육 신청서(붙임2) 작성 후 hrcedu@snu.ac.kr로 신청

컴퓨터공학부 개설 전필 과목의 경우 아래와 같이 시행하고 있으니 확인하여 주시기 바랍니다. - 수강신청 변경 기간 이후 수강취소 시 교원 승인으로 일괄 적용함.- 개강 2주 이후에는 수강취소 불가- 수강신청 변경 기간 중에는 동일하게 승인 없이 변경 가능- (추가) 2025학년도 2학기부터는 컴퓨터공학부 개설 전필 과목에 한하여 수강신청 변경 기간 중 발생하는 취소 여석은 선착순 신청이 불가능하며, 정원외신청자 중 선별하여 승인할 예정(졸업예정자 중 주전공, 복수전공, 부전공, 연합전공 순)임.(수강신청 금전 거래 방지 등 목적)​* 컴퓨터공학부 전필 과목 리스트 *교과목번호(Course Code)강좌번호(Course Number)교과구분(Course Type)교과목명(Course Title, KOR)교과목명(Course Title, ENG)비고4190.101001전필(Major Required)이산수학Discrete MathematicsM1522.000600001전필(Major Required)컴퓨터프로그래밍Computer Programming M1522.000700001전필(Major Required)논리설계Logic Design M1522.000900 001전필(Major Required)자료구조Data Structures 4190.308001전필(Major Required)컴퓨터구조Computer Architecture M1522.000800001전필(Major Required)시스템프로그래밍Systems Programming 4190.407001전필(Major Required)알고리즘Algorithms 감사합니다.

[Press Release] SNU RTOSLab Takes Second Place in International AI Model Optimization Competition
Analyzing the architecture of a domestically developed NPU Improving computational efficiency through software optimization Achieving a throughput of 821.1 tokens per second ▲ Graduate students from the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering at Seoul National University took second place in the NPU Model Optimization Competition at the IEEE/ACM International Symposium on Microarchitecture (MICRO 2026), held in Athens, Greece, on November 1 (local time) A research team from Seoul National University took second place in an international competition focused on optimizing artificial intelligence (AI) models. According to Seoul National University on November 2, Jae-min Yoon and Jangwoon Park, master’s students at the Real-Time Operating Systems Laboratory (RTOSLab) in the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering, placed second in the NPU Model Optimization Competition held at the IEEE/ACM International Symposium on Microarchitecture (MICRO 2026). The competition challenged participants to accelerate Google’s multimodal AI model “Gemma 4 12B” on “Renegade (RNGD),” a neural processing unit (NPU) developed by AI semiconductor company FuriosaAI. In the first round, more than 100 teams worked on optimizing three core operations of the AI model. The 64 teams that advanced to the second round improved the model’s overall performance in actual service conditions. The SNU RTOSLab team designed a new inference engine that processes requests from multiple users simultaneously. The team minimized waiting time by preparing the next task while the NPU was executing the current one and improved processing efficiency by reducing computational precision while maintaining accuracy. Through these methods, the team improved the inference engine’s performance more than fivefold, ultimately achieving a throughput of 821.1 tokens per second. Professor Seongsoo Hong of the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering at Seoul National University emphasized, “The service performance of AI semiconductors depends heavily on software, not just the chips themselves. This achievement is meaningful because the team directly analyzed the hardware architecture of a domestically developed AI semiconductor and improved its performance by optimizing the software accordingly.” Source: https://m.sedaily.com/article/20109132 Translated by: JungHwan Kim, English Editor of the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering, onzdavid@snu.ac.kr...
Nov 2, 2026
[Press Release] “AI That Knows How to Forget” and “Robots That Can Feel”: Two SNU Projects Selected for Samsung’s Research Funding for Future Technology Program
▲ (From left) Professor Yongtaek Hong, Professor Jonghyun Choi, and Professor Se Young Chun of the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering at Seoul National University. Two research projects, one by the joint research team of Professors Yongtaek Hong and Jonghyun Choi and the other by Professor Se Young Chun’s research team in the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering at Seoul National University, have been selected for Samsung’s Research Funding for Future Technology Program for the second half of 2026. The two teams will address key challenges in robotic sensing and artificial intelligence (AI) memory, respectively. By studying robots that independently adjust their actions based on tactile information and AI that selectively removes unnecessary memories, they plan to develop next-generation AI technologies that respond flexibly to real-world environments. In particular, the joint project led by Professors Hong and Choi was the only project selected in the program’s next-generation robotics category. [Robots That Adjust Their Grip When Objects Slip and Let Go When They Are Hot: Developing Robots with Tactile Physical AI] The joint research team led by Professors Hong and Choi will study next-generation robotic AI that enables robots to touch and feel objects and independently adjust their actions, much like humans. Even without looking, people adjust their grip when a cup slips from their hand and reflexively withdraw their hand upon touching a hot object. They assess situations and change their actions based on sensory information obtained through their skin. Robots, on the other hand, have made significant advances in recognizing their surroundings and planning tasks using cameras and AI. However, when grasping objects or assembling components, the contact area between the hand and the object is often hidden from the camera. This makes it difficult to accurately detect slight slipping, abnormal forces, or dangerous temperature changes. These “sensory blind spots” can lead to task failures or component damage when assembling small parts or handling unfamiliar objects. They remain a major limitation to robots performing tasks with human-like precision in diverse environments. To address these challenges, the team will develop high-resolution electronic skin that deforms flexibly like human skin while precisely detecting pressure, temperature, and heat flow. The team will also study AI technology that combines tactile information from the electronic skin with camera images and language information, enabling robots to decide how to act independently. The key is to combine Professor Hong’s flexible and stretchable sensor technology with Professor Choi’s robotic AI technology. The team plans to develop technology that allows robots to adjust their grip when they detect an object slipping, independently release hot components to prevent damage, and modify their posture and movements when unexpected resistance occurs during assembly. The team will also test whether robots can understand contact conditions hidden from view and respond appropriately, even when handling objects they have not previously been trained on or working in unpredictable manufacturing environments. The goal is to establish a foundation for core intelligence technologies for both industrial robots performing precision manufacturing and assembly tasks and humanoid robots handling diverse objects safely and delicately. [Should AI Forget to Become Smarter? Developing AI with “Selective Forgetting”] Professor Chun’s research team will study AI capable of “selective forgetting,” identifying and removing unnecessary memories. Rather than remembering everything, the human brain lets unimportant details fade while selecting and retaining memories that will be useful in the future. Research in memory science has suggested that selective forgetting reduces interference from outdated information and supports generalization by preserving the essential elements of experiences. In contrast, AI research has largely focused on retaining as much learned information as possible without forgetting it. However, AI lacks the ability to independently decide what to forget. As information accumulates, important clues can become buried, or existing capabilities can deteriorate while the AI learns new information. The team will investigate the hypothesis that selectively removing unnecessary memories can improve AI performance. It plans to develop technology that enables AI to assess the future usefulness of its memories, retain those that are needed, and remove those that are unnecessary. The technology will be progressively evaluated in multimodal AI agents that understand both images and language and sustain extended conversations and tasks. Through this research, the team aims to lay the groundwork for AI assistants that maintain context throughout lengthy tasks and on-device AI that operates with less memory and power. Media Coverage External Link: Edaily: https://www.edaily.co.kr/News/Read?newsId=03670326645612264&mediaCodeNo=257 Seoul Economic Daily: https://www.sedaily.com/article/20101652?ref=naver The Robot News: https://www.irobotnews.com/news/articleView.html?idxno=48946 VERITAS: https://www.veritas-a.com/news/articleView.html?idxno=631374 Source: https://ece.snu.ac.kr/ece/news?md=v&bbsidx=58181 Translated by: JungHwan Kim, English Editor of the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering, onzdavid@snu.ac.kr...

[Other] Late Chairman Jeong-sik Kim Selected for the Proud SNU Alumni Award, Honoring His Vision for the Haedong Advanced Engineering Center
Late Chairman Jeong-sik Kim Selected as a Recipient of the 36th Proud SNU Alumni Award ▲ The late Jeong-sik Kim, former Chairman of Daeduck Electronics Co., Ltd. and former Chairman of the Haedong Science and Culture Foundation. In October 2026, the late Chairman Jeong-sik Kim, who graduated from the Department of Communication Engineering at Seoul National University’s College of Engineering in 1956, was selected as a recipient of the 36th Proud SNU Alumni Award. In February 2019, Chairman Kim donated his personal funds toward the construction and operation of the Haedong Advanced Engineering Center to foster interdisciplinary talent and advance artificial intelligence (AI), a key field in the Fourth Industrial Revolution. His donations to his alma mater, including the Haedong Academic Building Construction Fund, the Jeong-sik Kim Development Fund, and the Daeduck Electronics Academic Fund, totaled KRW 65.7 billion, the largest individual donation in Seoul National University’s history. In particular, his full support for the establishment of the Haedong Advanced Engineering Center, an AI research and education platform at Seoul National University, laid the foundation for interdisciplinary research and the development of future talent across engineering fields, including robotics, semiconductors, energy, and biotechnology. These contributions have been highly recognized for advancing national science and technology and strengthening industrial competitiveness. Seoul National University’s College of Engineering deeply honors Chairman Kim’s lifelong dedication to his alma mater and society and his commitment to giving. Carrying forward his noble spirit, the college will further strengthen its efforts to nurture creative engineering talent who will lead the future. [ECE Department] Professor Kyomin Jung’s Research Team Has More Than 20 Papers Accepted Annually at Leading AI Conferences for Two Consecutive Years
▲ Professor Kyomin Jung and researchers from the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering at Seoul National University. (Top row, from left) Professor Kyomin Jung, Nakyeong Yang, Minbeom Kim, Yerin Hwang, and Yunah Jang. (Bottom row, from left) Seongho Joo, Minha Jhang, Kyeongman Park, Junseok Kim, and Dohyung Kim. Professor Kyomin Jung’s research team in the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering at Seoul National University has had a total of 44 papers accepted at leading international artificial intelligence (AI) conferences over two years: 21 in 2025 and 23 in 2026. The Machine Intelligence Lab (MILAB), led by Professor Jung, has conducted extensive research on reasoning and training efficiency in large language models (LLMs), consistency evaluation of AI-generated images, and AI agent safety. In 2026, the team had nine papers accepted at EMNLP and five at ACL, two leading international conferences in natural language processing. It also had one paper accepted at NeurIPS, three at ICML, and one at ICLR, major international conferences in machine learning. With two additional papers accepted at EACL and one each at AAAI and ECCV, the team achieved a total of 23 accepted papers this year. In 2025, the team had 21 papers accepted: nine at EMNLP, six at NAACL, two at ACL, and one each at NeurIPS, ICLR, CIKM, and COLING. This marks two consecutive years with more than 20 accepted papers annually. Among the representative studies, “Evaluating Visual Narrative Coherence in Story Visualization via Diversified Storylines,” by Minha Jhang and colleagues, was selected for an oral presentation at ACL 2026. The study proposed a method for evaluating visual narrative coherence across sequences of AI-generated images. It comprehensively assesses not only whether individual images match their descriptions, but also whether characters and backgrounds remain consistent across scenes. In experiments comparing AI evaluations with human judgments, the method achieved approximately 95% of the agreement level observed between human evaluators. The technology is expected to help identify inconsistencies between scenes in AI-generated picture books and story content, reducing the burden of manual review for creators. “Beyond Normalization: Rethinking the Partition Function as a Difficulty Scheduler for RLVR,” by Dohyung Kim and colleagues, was accepted at ICML 2026. The study proposed “PACED-RL,” a reinforcement learning technique that efficiently improves AI’s mathematical reasoning and code generation capabilities. It uses the partition function, traditionally used mainly to normalize computed values, to estimate the difficulty of individual problems and concentrate computational resources on problems that are beneficial for learning. In experiments, accuracy on a mathematical reasoning evaluation increased from 23.9% with the existing method to 28.7%. In a code generation evaluation, the technique reduced the training time needed to reach the existing method’s peak performance by approximately half. By reducing the time and cost of AI training, the technology is expected to ease the burden of AI development for university laboratories and small and medium-sized enterprises. The team also achieved results in improving AI agent safety. “LiSA: Lifelong Safety Adaptation via Conservative Policy Induction,” by Minbeom Kim and colleagues, was accepted at NeurIPS 2026. The technology continuously improves AI agents’ safety judgments based on errors reported by users. It derives rules for privacy protection and task execution from cases of incorrect judgment, then selects and applies rules supported by sufficient evidence. This allows AI agents to respond to organization-specific security requirements and emerging risks without retraining the AI model. In experiments, LiSA distinguished safe actions from unsafe ones more accurately than existing methods. It also maintained better performance than the comparison methods even when 20% of user feedback contained incorrect information. The technology is expected to help prevent personal information leaks from enterprise AI assistants and improve the safety of workflow automation. ▲ How LiSA improves AI agent safety. The technology derives general safety policies and context-specific rules from user-reported errors, verifies the reliability of the general policies, and uses them to make safety judgments. The team is also improving the reliability and practical applications of AI through research on personalized guidance in long-term conversations, privacy-preserving language generation, and the evaluation of AI’s ability to follow instructions. The team has also achieved results in training researchers. Dr. Yerin Hwang, who earned her Ph.D. in August 2026, was appointed as a professor in the Department of Computer Science and Artificial Intelligence at Dongguk University in September of the same year. Dr. Minbeom Kim, who completed his doctoral studies during the same period, received the Best Ph.D. Dissertation Award from Seoul National University’s Interdisciplinary Program in Artificial Intelligence and is currently working at the SK hynix AI Research Center. Professor Jung, who supervised the research, stated, “At a time when language-processing-based AI research, including LLMs, is becoming increasingly important, it is encouraging that we have been able to lead research by actively presenting papers at the world’s leading conferences over several years. Through creative and challenging research, we will continue contributing to the AI research capabilities of SNU’s College of Engineering and Korea’s academic and industrial communities.” The research introduced in this article was supported by the National Research Foundation of Korea (NRF) and the Institute of Information & Communications Technology Planning & Evaluation (IITP), with funding from the Ministry of Science and ICT. [References] Laboratory website: https://milab.snu.ac.kr/ Representative Paper 1: Evaluating Visual Narrative Coherence in Story Visualization via Diversified Storylines (ACL 2026, Oral) DOI: https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.acl-long.1578 Representative Paper 2: Beyond Normalization: Rethinking the Partition Function as a Difficulty Scheduler for RLVR (ICML 2026) DOI (arXiv): https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2602.12642 Representative Paper 3: LiSA: Lifelong Safety Adaptation via Conservative Policy Induction (NeurIPS 2026) DOI (arXiv): https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2605.14454 [Contact] Professor Kyomin Jung, Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering, Seoul National University / +82-2-880-1937 / kjung@snu.ac.kr Source: https://ece.snu.ac.kr/ece/news?md=v&bbsidx=58178 Translated by: JungHwan Kim, English Editor of the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering, onzdavid@snu.ac.kr...

Oct 15, 2026
[Other] Professor Jung-Ik Ha Receives the 36th Science and Technology Excellent Paper Award
Professor Jung-Ik Ha of the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering at Seoul National University received the 36th Science and Technology Excellent Paper Award on September 30. Established by the Korean Federation of Science and Technology Societies (KOFST), the Science and Technology Excellent Paper Award is presented to scientists and engineers who publish outstanding papers in domestic academic journals through creative research. Each year, papers nominated by academic societies are selected through an in-depth evaluation of their creativity and academic contributions, and the award is presented to the principal investigator of each selected paper. In the award-winning paper, Professor Ha proposed a new control method for unbalanced power systems. Unlike conventional approaches that use phase voltages, the proposed method uses line-to-line voltages to improve power-control performance during transient conditions. Oct 14, 2026
[Press Release] NIA Launches Domestic AI-RAN Demonstration Project on University Campuses
▲ Participants at the launch ceremony for the “K-AI Network Competitiveness Enhancement” project, held on October 1st at the conference room of the New Media Communications Joint Research Institute at Seoul National University. The National Information Society Agency (NIA) and the Ministry of Science and ICT (MSIT) held a launch ceremony for the “K-AI Network Competitiveness Enhancement” project at Seoul National University on October 1st, initiating next-generation AI network demonstrations. For the project’s core initiative, the “Campus AI Network Demonstration,” KRW 2.2 billion in government funding will be invested in 2026. NewGens will serve as the lead organization, with Seoul National University, KAIST, Yonsei University, and HFR participating in the project through December 2027. The consortium will establish AI-RAN demonstration environments based on domestically developed equipment and solutions across the campuses of Seoul National University, KAIST, and Yonsei University. The three universities will be connected through the Korea Advanced Research Network (KOREN) to verify RAN performance and interoperability, as well as demonstrate services in the fields of physical AI and robotics. Seoul National University will demonstrate an integrated campus guidance service using AI hologram technology, while KAIST will demonstrate an on-campus transportation and delivery service based on autonomous driving robots. Yonsei University will develop an indoor autonomous patrol and escort guidance service using multimodal quadruped robots. Through the project, NIA plans to identify models for optimizing RAN performance using AI and demonstrating physical AI services, thereby supporting the wider adoption of domestic AI network technologies in industry and laying the groundwork for overseas expansion. At the launch ceremony, NIA and the National Robotics Research Institute at Seoul National University declared a vision for collaboration between AI-RAN and physical AI. Participants also discussed the potential for linking AI-RAN demonstration environments with physical AI research, including robotics. NIA President Hyeong-Cheol Kim stated, “By connecting the research capabilities of universities with the technological capabilities of industry, we will strengthen the competitiveness of domestic AI-RAN technology and support the expansion of demonstration results accumulated on university campuses into industries and overseas markets.” Source: https://www.joongang.co.kr/article/25466687 Translated by: JungHwan Kim, English Editor of the Department of Electrical and Computer Engineering, onzdavid@snu.ac.kr...
